So... here I am.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not waking up and your family being better off without you?\n\nPatient: I suppose it's been... about a year and a month. Ever since my bowling partner passed away. It's just been going on since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you used to like during this past year?\n\nPatient: Not really. I suppose I used to look forward to things... playing bridge, the garden. The cooking at church. Cubs games. But... I don't know. Now I mostly just sit in my chair. I just can't make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would end your life if you decided to go through with not waking up?\n\nPatient: I haven't... I haven't really made a plan or anything. Like I said before, it's just... I just think everyone would be better off if I didn't wake up. I just don't see the need to see another winter. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No... like I just said a minute ago. I don't care about any of it now. The bridge, the garden, the Cubs games... I just sit in my chair. There's nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to or having trouble getting out of your chair?\n\nPatient: I suppose. My wife says I do... she tells me I move like I'm walking through deep snow. It just... takes me so long now to get dressed. Or even cross the kitchen. I usually just give up before I do much of anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I don't really pay attention to the scale anymore. I haven't noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your clothes are looser on you than they used to be?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mostly just stay in my pajamas or my robe these days, so... I wouldn't really know.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about a specific way you would end your life if you decided to go through with it?\n\nPatient: Not a specific way... no. I just... I keep thinking my wife and the kids would be better off if I just didn't wake up. I've been saying lately that I don't really need to see another winter... but I haven't figured out how. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so... my wife tells me I move like I'm walking through deep snow. I don't really know, but... just getting dressed or trying to cross the kitchen... it takes so long now. I usually just give up before I've done much of anything.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I used to... I liked playing bridge. And the garden. Used to help with the cooking at church, sometimes went to Cubs games... but now I just... sit in my chair. I can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worse now than you were a few months ago?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... it's just been a steady decline. I don't really keep track of the months. About the snow. It's... it's just hard to move. I try to get dressed, and it just takes so much out of me. I end up just stopping.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I used to... I used to look forward to things. Playing bridge. Like I said a minute ago... it's the exact opposite. Just trying to walk across the kitchen takes so long now. I usually just give up before I've really done anything. So... no, I don't have trouble sitting still.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgeting or have an urge to move around?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? No. I don't.
